<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which molecular function is the best match for this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Ubiquitin-protein ligase activity<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Ubiquitin-specific protease activity<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Protein kinase activity<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br># Analysis of Protein Molecular Function<br><br>## Step 1: Sequence Analysis<br><br>The protein (~530 amino acids) contains:<br>- An N-terminal region with multiple cysteine residues suggestive of zinc-binding motifs<br>- **Four to five tandem cyclophilin/peptidyl-prolyl isomerase (PPIase) domains** identified by the characteristic `GG...CYDP` or `GG...SYDP` motifs at positions:<br>  - `GGCERVGGFNLPYTECYDP`<br>  - `GGYDGQNRLSSVECYDSF`<br>  - `GGGPDDNTCSDKVQSYDP`<br>  - `GGLTKAVYCYDPV`<br>  - `GGRRENGEATDTILCYDP`<br><br>## Step 2: Domain Architecture Assessment<br><br>This architecture — multiple substrate-binding cyclophilin domains + an N-terminal interaction/binding domain — is consistent with a protein that functions through **protein-protein interaction** rather than catalysis.<br><br>## Step 3: Evaluating Each Option<br><br>&#124; Option &#124; Required Domain &#124; Present? &#124;<br>&#124;--------&#124;----------------&#124;----------&#124;<br>&#124; A. Ubiquitin-protein ligase &#124; RING, HECT, U-box, or RBR catalytic domain &#124; ❌ Not detected &#124;<br>&#124; B. Ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor &#124; Substrate-recognition domains (no catalytic domain needed) &#124; ✓ Cyclophilin domains serve as substrate-binding modules &#124;<br>&#124; C. Ubiquitin-specific protease &#124; USP protease domain (~500 aa) &#124; ❌ Not detected &#124;<br>&#124; D. Protein kinase &#124; Kinase motifs (VAIK, HRD, DFG) &#124; ❌ Not detected &#124;<br><br>## Step 4: Reasoning<br><br>Options A, C, and D all require specific **catalytic domains** (RING/HECT/U-box for ligases, USP for proteases, kinase domain for kinases) — none of which are present. Option B (adaptor activity) describes a protein that **recognizes and presents substrates** to catalytic components using binding domains (here, the cyclophilin domains that bind proline-containing substrates), without performing catalysis itself. This is the only option consistent with the observed domain architecture.<br><br>## answer: B</td>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> A protein with the sequence shown is reported to associate with CUL3 and RBX1 as part of a larger protein complex. Which molecular function best describes the protein itself, rather than assuming it performs every activity of the complex?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> Ubiquitin-protein ligase activity<br><br><strong>B.</strong> Ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity<br><br><strong>C.</strong> Ubiquitin-specific protease activity<br><br><strong>D.</strong> Protein kinase activity<br><br><strong>Skills:</strong> GO_molecular_function_0145:2<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: <br><br>**Sequence Analysis:**<br>- The N-terminal region (~residues 1-150) contains a **BTB/POZ domain**, identifiable by the conserved hydrophobic pattern and structural motifs (e.g., &quot;CHRAVLSACSSYFRAMFCNDHRES,&quot; &quot;PCNCLGIQRFADTHSLKTLFTKCK&quot;).<br>- The C-terminal region contains multiple **Kelch-like β-propeller repeats** (6 Kelch repeats), recognizable by the characteristic &quot;GG&quot; motifs and pattern: e.g., &quot;IVVVGGCERVGGFNLPY,&quot; &quot;KYVVGGYDGQNRLSS,&quot; &quot;FVIGGGPDDNTCSD,&quot; &quot;KYILGGRRENGEATD,&quot; etc.<br><br>**Biological Context:**<br>This is a **BTB-Kelch protein** that associates with CUL3 (scaffold) and RBX1 (RING protein). In Cullin-RING E3 ligase (CRL) complexes:<br>- CUL3 = scaffold<br>- RBX1 = recruits E2 and mediates ubiquitin transfer<br>- BTB-Kelch protein = **substrate adaptor** (BTB domain binds CUL3; Kelch repeats bind substrates)<br><br>**Evaluating Options:**<br>- **A (Ubiquitin-protein ligase activity):** This is the activity of the *entire complex* (primarily via RBX1&#x27;s RING domain). The BTB-Kelch protein itself does not catalyze ubiquitin transfer.<br>- **B (Ubiquitin-like ligase-substrate adaptor activity):** Perfectly describes the BTB-Kelch protein&#x27;s role — it recruits substrates via Kelch repeats and tethers them to CUL3 via the BTB domain, functioning as an adaptor.<br>- **C (Ubiquitin-specific protease activity):** No catalytic protease domain is present.<br>- **D (Protein kinase activity):** No kinase motifs (VAIK, HRD, DFG) are present.<br><br>The question emphasizes describing the protein&#x27;s *own* function, not the complex&#x27;s overall activity. The BTB-Kelch protein is an adaptor, not a catalytic enzyme.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>

The C4 zinc site in such domains typically consists of two close cysteine pairs (e.g., CX₂C...CX₃C) separated by a loop, which fold to bring all four sulfur atoms into tetrahedral coordination around the zinc ion. Option B provides exactly this architecture.<br><br>1. **reasoning:** Option B (Cys21, Cys24, Cys107, Cys110) provides two geometrically favorable cysteine pairs: C21-x₂-C24 (CX₂C) and C107-x₃-C110 (CX₃C). This is the canonical arrangement for a C4-type zinc-binding site in RING-type domains, where two cysteine pairs coordinate a single zinc ion in tetrahedral geometry.
